# Source 294: karpathy

Reviewed 2026-10-25, Europe/Warsaw. [Source](https://recommentions.com/andrej-karpathy/books/). New canonical source; tracking query removed.

## Scope and method

The user explicitly requested reuse of existing decisions and detailed assessment only for new books. Existing gates, attainment, deductions and R&D are retained. Only distinct source coverage changes REL. [Extraction manifest](../data/shelves_261025/inputs.json); [source/title inventory](../data/shelves_261025/candidates.json); [identity matches](../data/shelves_261025/matches.json). This intake is in progress: new titles remain in [the pending queue](../data/shelves_261025/pending.json). No completeness claim for their assessment.

Expanded the embedded HTML template behind “Load 299 more books”: all 349 list entries captured.

<a id="shelves-microcosm-zimmer"></a>

### New: Microcosm: E. coli and the New Science of Life — Carl Zimmer

Eligible explanatory science narrative, not a laboratory curriculum. Disease is an application, not the dominant topic, so T11=0; the central subject is cellular mechanisms and discovery, so T12=0. No serious veto established: Nataro identifies specific mistakes and omissions, not failure of the central experimental account. Do not confuse this with George Gilder's different Microcosm. Mandatory Karpathy coverage; expert and reader praise is documented but not unanimous.

Status: recommended. Verdict: Selective; confidence moderate. 

| Criterion | Linear attainment | Contribution | Assessment |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| E | 17.00 / 20 | 14.45 | JCI reviewer James Nataro praises the account but identifies incorrect flagellar illustrations, peptide charge and thin coverage of commensal strains; these limit E without invalidating the whole narrative. |
| S | 11.00 / 17 | 7.12 | Connects molecular machinery, experimental methods, evolution and biotechnology; industrial institutions receive less attention. |
| U | 10.00 / 15 | 10.00 | Useful models of feedback, resource allocation and experimental inference; transfer to engineering is an interpretation, not an experimentally demonstrated equivalence. |
| M | 10.00 / 12 | 10.00 | Explains gene regulation, bacterial exchange and adaptation through experiments and cellular processes. |
| D | 8.00 / 10 | 6.40 | D=8/10, moderate confidence: contents, Nataro's review, Science News and Karpathy's reading report. Gene regulation, motility, cooperation and engineering add distinct learning. Some readers report a slow pace and uneven transitions; no full-text density audit. |
| R | 8.00 / 9 | 8.00 | Experimental history and basic mechanisms remain durable; contemporary biotechnology examples reflect 2008 knowledge. |
| F | 5.00 / 7 | 5.00 | Strong for biological machinery and scientific method; less direct than industrial or computing history. |
| A | 4.00 / 5 | 4.00 | Established science reporter; substantive assessment by an E. coli specialist supports authority while exposing limitations. |
| N | 2.50 / 3 | 2.50 | A single model organism gives the narrative coherence; the final applications broaden its scope. |
| P | 1.50 / 2 | 1.50 | Generally precise and accessible explanation, with occasional literary flourishes. |

### New: Gods and Robots: Myths, Machines, and Ancient Dreams of Technology — Adrienne Mayor

Not fiction or devotional writing: this is historical analysis of myths and technology. H7 does not exclude the main technology-imagination inquiry. V9 applies despite substantial specialist praise for research and presentation. Considered defense: material components and breakdowns in Talos support some mechanical readings, and Mayor acknowledges opposing views; the reviewer remains unconvinced about broad ancient assumptions. Counterfactual score preserves this foundational uncertainty. Ancient and medieval material dominates; endpoint 1200 yields capped H13. T11=T12=0.

Status: vetoed. Verdict: Vetoed; counterfactual score; confidence moderate. Veto V9; confidence high. The recurring interpretation that ancient artificial beings were understood chiefly as manufactured technology rests on contested reconstruction of ancient cognition. Bosak-Schroeder's detailed specialist review challenges this foundation and cites prior scholarship supporting magical interpretations. Mayor's component-based evidence persuades the reviewer in some cases, but does not establish the broader framing. The criticism concerns the central lens, not merely omissions. Counterfactual score only.

### Hard exclusion: Vision: A Computational Investigation into the Human Representation and Processing of Visual Information — David Marr

The main body develops specialist computational representations and methods for image structure, stereopsis, surface reconstruction and shape recognition. H3 applies before scoring; prestige and accessible introductory ideas do not override it. Confidence: high. Sources: 294. ### New: The World After Capital — Albert Wenger

Mandatory Karpathy review. Published paperback exists, so H1 does not apply to the earlier online draft. Earliest public book draft was being read by May 2016; original-production year uses 2016 with moderate confidence, not the 2021 print date. Political economy and philosophy are central, T12=-10. Neither reform advocacy nor humanism establishes ideological extremism. Meditation is subsidiary, not the dominant purpose. V9 concerns the core scarcity transition; counterfactual scoring retains both criticism and the practical value of platform interoperability proposals.

Status: vetoed. Verdict: Vetoed; counterfactual score; confidence moderate. Veto V9; confidence high. The central transition thesis assumes sufficient physical capital and that reallocating attention can sustain a knowledge economy. Economist Luigi Zingales and financial journalist Bethany McLean directly challenge the adequacy, distribution and incentive premises, not merely details. Their discussion also considers Wenger's defense and supports some platform proposals. These unresolved foundational assumptions meet V9 despite useful subsidiary ideas. Counterfactual score only.

### New: The World the Plague Made: The Black Death and the Rise of Europe — James Belich

Mandatory Karpathy review. Explanatory global economic history, not medical advice or specialist training. Disease is the initiating and recurring central factor, T11=-10; trade, technology and economic transformation organize the account, so T12=0 despite imperial applications. Central period roughly 1350-1800, endpoint 1800. Cowen challenges the evidence for sustained living-standard gains and the simple per-capita wealth inference. Stoll and Mazo praise the historical synthesis, while Belich explicitly treats plague as one important cause rather than the sole explanation. The inspected criticism creates substantial uncertainty but does not establish V9 with high confidence after these defenses. Reader response is mixed; not selected for the praised-book quota.

### New: The Next 100 Years: A Forecast for the 21st Century — George Friedman

Mandatory Karpathy review. Speculative nonfiction is not fiction merely because scenarios are implausible. V9 applies to the forecasting foundation, not to the existence of forecasting as a genre. Friedman explains in his Carnegie Council remarks that the purpose is to identify durable forces; Kirkus finds useful geopolitical reasoning. These defenses support subsidiary value but do not validate the detailed forecast. Global influence prevents an automatic US-only deduction; geopolitics and war are central, T12=-10. No historical endpoint deduction for future scenarios. Not selected for praise.

Status: vetoed. Verdict: Vetoed; counterfactual score; confidence moderate. Veto V9; confidence high. The central century-long forecast depends on selected geopolitical constraints being sufficiently determinative to predict detailed events. Forecasting specialist Philip Tetlock, in the review excerpt reproduced by international-relations scholar Daniel Drezner, criticizes its reliance on privileged pundit judgment rather than demonstrated predictive method. The criticism concerns the book's foundation; acknowledging that details may be wrong does not resolve it. Counterfactual score only.

Mandatory Karpathy review. Comparative European history and geopolitical analysis, not single-nation history or a specialist curriculum. ### New: Disunited Nations: The Scramble for Power in an Ungoverned World — Peter Zeihan

Mandatory Karpathy review. Same work as the source's alternate subtitle Succeeding in a World Where No One Gets Along; not Sean Byrnes's distinct book. Speculative nonfiction passes hard exclusions. V9 concerns the causal foundation rather than a particular political position or prediction. Kirkus strongly praises the geographic and demographic account; the useful comparative material is retained in the counterfactual score. World-wide consequences prevent T11; geopolitical power is central, T12=-10. No H13 for forward scenarios. Not selected despite high reader ratings.

Status: vetoed. Verdict: Vetoed; counterfactual score; confidence moderate. Veto V9; confidence high. The recurring forecast assumes that US withdrawal necessarily ends the international order and produces a largely ungoverned world. International-relations scholar Herman Salton directly identifies this foundational simplification and the assumed rather than established condition of non-governance. Country-level geographic detail does not establish that central causal chain. Favorable reception of the book's exposition does not resolve this criticism. Counterfactual score only.

### New: UFOs: Generals, Pilots, and Government Officials Go on the Record — Leslie Kean

Mandatory Karpathy coverage. Nonfiction investigation, not fiction or religious doctrine. Publisher-reproduced praise from Kaku, Lane and other scientists was considered. Kean's 2010 reply says she concentrates on the thoroughly investigated residual five percent and seeks investigation rather than proof of extraterrestrial origin. That defense does not resolve the documented problem of omitted alternatives. V9 does not assume every sighting has been explained. No thematic deduction: anomalous observations, not military doctrine, are central. Not selected.

Status: vetoed. Verdict: Vetoed; counterfactual score; confidence moderate. Veto V9; confidence high. The book's residual-unexplained-case argument relies on discounting ordinary explanations and treating witness rank as unusually strong evidence. Robert Sheaffer's specific critique identifies omitted competing analyses of central cases and contrary testimony from Hynek on pilot reliability. These objections concern the evidential selection underlying the central argument, not simply the possibility of extraterrestrial life. Counterfactual score only.

Mandatory Karpathy coverage. General explanation of a biomedical research program, not specialist instruction or personal treatment advice; H3 and V7 do not apply. Bains praises its scientific stimulus while rejecting its extreme version. De Grey defends repair as engineering rather than mere treatment of symptoms. The Technology Review challenge did not prove the program unworthy of debate; that is a stronger test than V9, which does not require refutation. Criticism predates the book but concerns the same seven-part framework described in Bains's book review. The veto does not deny the value of research into aging. T11 applies to the central prevention of age-related disease and debility. Not selected.

Status: vetoed. Verdict: Vetoed; counterfactual score; confidence moderate. Veto V9; confidence high. The central promise requires a proposed set of damage-repair interventions to be sufficiently complete and jointly feasible to produce repeated rejuvenation. Warner and colleagues' EMBO critique directly disputes those assumptions and the extrapolation from component proposals to comprehensive reversal. The book makes this disputed program its organizing argument, not an incidental speculation. Counterfactual score only.

Mandatory Karpathy coverage. Main purpose is explanatory historical modeling, not teaching mathematical proficiency; H3 does not apply. Mandatory Karpathy coverage; distinct from James Burnham's same-title book. Political and intellectual history passes hard exclusions. Anheier criticizes shallow explanatory breadth; Ceaser recognizes substantial scholarly discussion, and Wolpe identifies both acute observations and major disputes. The inspected evidence supports severe score limits but not a high-confidence foundational veto. Defense of liberalism is not itself V2 or V6; critique extends to right-wing tribalism as well. ### New: The Ultimate History of Video Games, Volume 1 — Steven L. Kent

Mandatory Karpathy coverage. History of creative technical production passes H7; not a games manual. Original production was The First Quarter (2000), revised and retitled in 2001; no reset of P14. This review concerns volume 1 only. Johnson acknowledges valuable interviews and broad chronological coverage, and readers strongly enjoy it; those defenses do not cure the evidence failures. US-centered reporting concerns an internationally influential industry, so T11 is not automatic. Technology production is central, not media commentary under T12. Not selected.

Status: vetoed. Verdict: Vetoed; counterfactual score; confidence moderate. Veto V1; confidence high. Specialist historical review by Ethan Johnson documents repeated source-handling failures: executive recollections and promotional interviews are used without adequate dating or challenge, and the Kassar dismissal narrative is treated as established despite contrary evidence. The problem affects the recurring account of industrial decisions and success, beyond incidental errata. Counterfactual score only.

### New: The Age of Cryptocurrency: How Bitcoin and Digital Money Are Challenging the Global Economic Order — Paul Vigna and Michael J. Casey

Mandatory Karpathy coverage. General history and analysis, not investment instructions or specialist training. Munger's positive review and Swanson's praise for the electronic-money history and prose preserve substantial counterfactual value. They do not establish the claimed removal of institutional trust. Veto concerns foundations, not cryptocurrency as a topic. Finance is central, T12=-10; global scope gives T11=0. Paperback subtitle changes Digital Money to the Blockchain; same work. Not selected.

Status: vetoed. Verdict: Vetoed; counterfactual score; confidence moderate. Veto V9; confidence high. The central transformation narrative relies on an overstated removal of trust and intermediaries. Eric Posner directly challenges that foundation: reliance shifts to software, governance and service providers. Tim Swanson's chapter-level critique independently disputes adoption metrics and the inference from the protocol to broad financial replacement. These are central causal claims, beyond merely outdated prices. Counterfactual score only.
